📗 主报告：综述 / Primary Reviews

Journal: Tropical Medicine and Health  |  Canonical publication date: 2026-07-28  |  DOI: 10.1186/s41182-026-01031-4  |  Date basis: online_date  |  Publication status: Current window  |  Citation: 2026, 54, 1
A 高优先级学术文献 · 综述A high priorityAcademic literature · Review

登革热、黄热病和西尼罗河病毒在几内亚传播的研究:对实现西芒杜 2040 计划卫生目标的影响

Study of the circulation of dengue, yellow fever, and West Nile viruses in Guinea: implications for achieving the health objectives of the Simandou 2040 program
作者： Mamadou Boundoukhoura Bah, Mariama Diallo, Alphonse Keller Konkon, Mamadou Morou Barry, Aissatou Boiro, Ibrahima Baϊlo Diallo, Ibrahima Sory Sow, Thierno Amadou Labe Balde, Salifou Talasson Bangoura, Kirill Zakharov
摘要中文翻译背景:通过节肢动物传播的登革热、黄热病和西尼罗河病毒等疾病给热带和亚热带地区带来了公共卫生问题。与许多西非国家一样,几内亚拥有有利于这些病毒传播的所有条件。其中包括当地的生态和气候,以及传播这些病毒的 媒介 的存在。尽管这些病毒具有医学重要性,但对其进行的研究有限,而且有关其传播的最新信息仍然很少。在此背景下,本研究检查了人类和 媒介 人群中这三种虫媒病毒(DENV、YFV 和 WNV)的 患病率。方法:本研究覆盖了几内亚的 18 个省,分布在该国的四个生态区。该研究于2024年5月27日至11月27日进行。在每个都道府县选定的卫生中心,从就诊并征得同意的1-90岁无症状患者身上采集5毫升静脉血样本。此外,通过“AMPLECTA MOSQUITO TRAP”光诱捕器收集蚊子。对血液样本进行了两次 ELISA 测试:第一次用于检测,第二次用于确认人血清中是否存在这些病毒。对蚊子样本进行分析以确定多样性,并在通过 RIBO-prep AmpliSens® 试剂盒提取病毒 RNA 后,采用实时 PCR 检查 媒介 中的病毒感染。结果:在 18 个调查县中暴露于所有三种病毒的血清学证据表明它们在全国范围内广泛分布。血清流行率 抗体的最高 血清流行率 出现在恩泽雷科雷 (43.9% [38.5-49.4%]),而黄热病病毒抗体的最高 血清流行率 出现在坎坎县 (54.7%;95% CI: 41.5-67.3)。在 Forecariah 中,高达 53.0%(95% CI:42.4-63.4)的参与者西尼罗河病毒抗体血清呈阳性。这些虫媒病毒的血清阳性在成人中更为常见。农民和牲畜饲养者中也观察到较高的登革热和黄热病抗体 血清流行率。媒介 中未检测到登革热或黄热病感染,而致倦库蚊种群则被发现感染了西尼罗河病毒。结论:这项研究提供了几内亚共和国所有生态区域暴露于登革热、黄热病和西尼罗河病毒的血清学证据。此外,在人群中检测到了针对这些病毒的抗体,并且在昆虫学调查期间发现了虫媒病毒阳性的蚊子库。在含有致倦库蚊的蚊子池中也检测到了西尼罗河病毒的证据,表明该物种在当地 传播 循环中可能发挥作用。成年农民和牲畜饲养者感染登革热和黄热病的风险似乎最大。这些结果强调了几内亚需要一个集成的虫媒病毒 监测 系统。该系统必须将血清学 监测 与昆虫学调查结合起来,以支持 Simandou 2040 计划的公共卫生目标。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本综述考察了几内亚 登革病毒 (DENV)、黄热病病毒 (YFV) 和西尼罗河病毒 (WNV) 的传播情况,重点关注其 患病率 在该国四个生态区的人类和 媒介 人群中的传播。核心问题是评估接触这些虫媒病毒的血清学证据并确定感染的危险因素。
证据基础与方法
这是一项于 2024 年 5 月至 11 月在几内亚 18 个省进行的横断面研究。从 1 岁至 90 岁的无症状患者中采集血液样本,并使用光诱捕器收集蚊子。使用 ELISA 进行血清学检测,并通过实时 PCR 分析蚊子样本。该研究涵盖了几内亚的所有四个生态区。
共识与结论
该研究证实了几内亚所有生态区域广泛暴露于 DENV、YFV 和 WNV 的血清学证据。DENV 最高的 血清流行率 位于 N'Zerekore (43.9%),YFV 位于 Kankan (54.7%),WNV 位于 Forecariah (53.0%)。成年人,特别是农民和牲畜饲养者,表现出较高的 DENV 和 YFV 血清阳性率。在致倦库蚊中检测到西尼罗河病毒,表明其在当地 传播 中的作用。
争议与缺口
尽管人类血清呈阳性,但在 媒介 中未检测到 DENV 或 YFV 感染,这表明对这些病毒参与 媒介 的了解存在差距。该研究并未解决 媒介 中 DENV/YFV 的缺失或沉默 传播 循环的可能性。此外,血清学 之外的人类感染缺乏分子证实,这代表了方法学上的局限性。
科研与实践启示
研究结果强调了几内亚需要一个综合虫媒病毒 监测 系统,将血清学 监测 与昆虫学调查相结合。该系统对于支持西芒杜 2040 计划的公共卫生目标至关重要。有针对性的干预措施应重点关注农民和畜牧业者等高风险群体。

Study of the circulation of dengue, yellow fever, and West Nile viruses in Guinea: implications for achieving the health objectives of the Simandou 2040 program

Authors: Mamadou Boundoukhoura Bah, Mariama Diallo, Alphonse Keller Konkon, Mamadou Morou Barry, Aissatou Boiro, Ibrahima Baϊlo Diallo, Ibrahima Sory Sow, Thierno Amadou Labe Balde, Salifou Talasson Bangoura, Kirill Zakharov
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND: Diseases such as dengue, yellow fever and West Nile virus, which are transmitted by arthropods, pose public health problems in tropical and subtropical regions. Like many West African countries, Guinea possesses all the conditions conducive to the circulation of these viruses. These include local ecology and climate, as well as the presence of vectors that transmit these viruses. Despite their medical importance, these viruses have received limited study, and up-to-date information on their circulation remains scarce. In this context, the present study examined the prevalence of these three arboviruses (DENV, YFV and WNV) in human and vector populations. METHODOLOGY: This study covered 18 Guinea prefectures, which are spread across the country's four ecological zones. The study was conducted from 27 May to 27 November 2024. In health centers selected in each prefecture, 5 ml samples of venous blood were collected from asymptomatic patients aged 1-90 years who had visited for consultations and had given their consent. Additionally, mosquitoes were collected via an "AMPLECTA MOSQUITO TRAP" light trap. Two ELISA tests were performed on the blood samples: the first for detection and the second for confirmation of the presence of these viruses in human serum. The mosquito samples were analyzed to determine diversity, and real-time PCR was employed to check for viral infection in the vectors following viral RNA extraction via the RIBO-prep AmpliSens® Kit. RESULTS: Serological evidence of exposure to all three viruses in the eighteen surveyed prefectures indicates their widespread distribution across the country. The highest seroprevalence of dengue virus antibodies was observed in N'Zerekore (43.9% [38.5-49.4%]), whereas the highest seroprevalence of yellow fever virus antibodies was recorded in Kankan Prefecture (54.7%; 95% CI: 41.5-67.3). In Forecariah, up to 53.0% (95% CI: 42.4-63.4) of participants were seropositive for West Nile virus antibodies. Seropositivity for these arboviruses was more frequently observed among adults. Higher seroprevalence of dengue and yellow fever antibodies was also observed among farmers and livestock breeders. No dengue or yellow fever infections were detected in the vectors, whereas the Culex quinquefasciatus population was found to be infected with WNV. CONCLUSION: This study provides serological evidence of exposure to dengue, yellow fever, and West Nile viruses across all the ecological regions of the Republic of Guinea. In addition, antibodies against these viruses were detected in human populations, and arbovirus-positive mosquito pools were identified during entomological investigations. Evidence of West Nile virus was also detected in mosquito pools containing Culex quinquefasciatus, suggesting a possible role of this species in the local transmission cycle. Adult farmers and livestock breeders appear to be at the greatest risk of contracting dengue and yellow fever. These results highlight the need for an integrated arbovirus surveillance system in Guinea. This system must combine serological surveillance with entomological surveys to support the public health objectives of the Simandou 2040 program.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This review examines the circulation of dengue virus (DENV), yellow fever virus (YFV), and West Nile virus (WNV) in Guinea, focusing on their prevalence in human and vector populations across the country's four ecological zones. The central question is to assess the serological evidence of exposure to these arboviruses and identify risk factors for infection.
Evidence base and method
This is a cross-sectional study conducted across 18 prefectures in Guinea from May to November 2024. Blood samples were collected from asymptomatic patients aged 1-90 years, and mosquitoes were collected using light traps. Serological testing was performed using ELISA, and mosquito samples were analyzed via real-time PCR. The study covered all four ecological zones of Guinea.
Consensus and conclusions
The study confirms widespread serological evidence of exposure to DENV, YFV, and WNV across all ecological regions of Guinea. The highest seroprevalence of DENV was in N'Zerekore (43.9%), YFV in Kankan (54.7%), and WNV in Forecariah (53.0%). Adults, particularly farmers and livestock breeders, showed higher seropositivity for DENV and YFV. WNV was detected in Culex quinquefasciatus mosquitoes, suggesting its role in local transmission.
Controversies and gaps
No DENV or YFV infections were detected in vectors, despite human seropositivity, indicating gaps in understanding vector involvement for these viruses. The study does not address the absence of DENV/YFV in vectors or the potential for silent transmission cycles. Additionally, the lack of molecular confirmation for human infections beyond serology represents a methodological limitation.
Research and practice implications
The findings highlight the need for an integrated arbovirus surveillance system in Guinea that combines serological surveillance with entomological surveys. This system is critical for supporting the public health objectives of the Simandou 2040 program. Targeted interventions should focus on high-risk groups such as farmers and livestock breeders.

登革热和基孔肯雅热感染的全球分子和血清学证据:对 158,608 名测试参与者的系统回顾和荟萃分析。

Global molecular and serological evidence of dengue and chikungunya infection: a systematic review and meta-analysis of 158,608 tested participants.
作者： Mohammed Alissa, Abdullah Alghamdi, Suad A. Alghamdi, Ghadah S. Abusalim, Abdulkarim S. Binshaya, Ghfren S. Aloraini, Tawfiq N. Juraybi, Meshari A. Alsuwat
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
登革病毒 (DENV) 和基孔肯雅病毒 (CHIKV) 是伊蚊传播的虫媒病毒,具有重叠的临床表现、共同的 媒介 以及在共流行环境中的巨大诊断挑战。
证据基础与方法
这项系统综述和荟萃分析综合了已发表的关于人类临床、监测 和社区研究人群中分子检测、血清学阳性和 DENV-CHIKV 双重阳性/合并感染的证据。根据 PRISMA 2020 指南,对 5 个书目数据库(PubMed/MEDLINE、Scopus、Web of Science、ScienceDirect 和 Google Scholar)和补充灰色文献/预印本来源进行了搜索,查找 1980 年 1 月 1 日至 2024 年 12 月 31 日发表的英语研究。符合条件的记录报告了使用公认的分子或方法提取的人类 DENV 和/或 CHIKV 的分子和分母。血清学测定。
共识与结论
The pooled DENV estimate was 13.8 % (95 % CI: 10.9–17.3; I 2 =99.0 %), with molecular and serological estimates of 13.1 % (95 % CI: 7.9–21.0) and 14.3 % (95 % CI: 10.2–19.8), respectively. The pooled CHIKV estimate was 14.0 % (95 % CI: 12.0–16.4; I 2 =97.5 %), with molecular and serological estimates of 9.8 and 15.7 %, respectively. DENV–CHIKV dual positivity/co-infection was 52.9 % (95 % CI: 48.7–57.1) among studies that tested and reported both outcomes. Available evidence indicates extensive but highly heterogeneous DENV and CHIKV positivity across selected clinical and surveillance populations.
争议与缺口
由于研究间存在显着的异质性、诊断变异性、暴发 周期抽样以及不均匀的地理代表性,因此应谨慎解释汇总估计值。国家层面的估计差异很大,应解释为现有研究的总结,而不是具有全国代表性的负担估计。漏斗图不对称性在 DENV 分析中具有统计学显着性,但在整体 CHIKV 分析中并不显着。没有可用的前瞻性 PROSPERO 或 OSF 协议注册。
科研与实践启示
研究结果支持在共同流行地区进行综合虫媒病毒 监测、多重诊断和 媒介 控制准备。

Global molecular and serological evidence of dengue and chikungunya infection: a systematic review and meta-analysis of 158,608 tested participants.

Authors: Mohammed Alissa, Abdullah Alghamdi, Suad A. Alghamdi, Ghadah S. Abusalim, Abdulkarim S. Binshaya, Ghfren S. Aloraini, Tawfiq N. Juraybi, Meshari A. Alsuwat
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Introduction Dengue virus (DENV) and chikungunya virus (CHIKV) are Aedes-borne arboviruses with overlapping clinical manifestations, shared vectors, and substantial diagnostic challenges in co-endemic settings. This systematic review and meta-analysis synthesized published evidence on molecular detection, serological positivity, and DENV–CHIKV dual positivity/co-infection in human clinical, surveillance, and community-based study populations. Content Following PRISMA 2020 guidance, five bibliographic databases (PubMed/MEDLINE, Scopus, Web of Science, ScienceDirect, and Google Scholar) and supplementary grey-literature/preprint sources were searched for English-language studies published from 1 January 1980 to 31 December 2024. No prospective PROSPERO or OSF protocol registration was available. Eligible records reported extractable numerators and denominators for DENV and/or CHIKV in humans using recognized molecular or serological assays. A total of 196 studies comprising 158,608 tested or suspected participants were included in the extraction table. The pooled CHIKV estimate was 14.0 % (95 % CI: 12.0–16.4; I 2 =97.5 %), with molecular and serological estimates of 9.8 and 15.7 %, respectively. The pooled DENV estimate was 13.8 % (95 % CI: 10.9–17.3; I 2 =99.0 %), with molecular and serological estimates of 13.1 % (95 % CI: 7.9–21.0) and 14.3 % (95 % CI: 10.2–19.8), respectively. DENV–CHIKV dual positivity/co-infection was 52.9 % (95 % CI: 48.7–57.1) among studies that tested and reported both outcomes. Country-level estimates varied widely and should be interpreted as summaries of available studies rather than nationally representative burden estimates. Funnel-plot asymmetry was statistically significant in DENV analyses but not in the overall CHIKV analysis. Summary Available evidence indicates extensive but highly heterogeneous DENV and CHIKV positivity across selected clinical and surveillance populations. The pooled estimates should be interpreted cautiously because of substantial between-study heterogeneity, diagnostic variability, outbreak-period sampling, and uneven geographic representation. Outlook The findings support integrated arboviral surveillance, multiplex diagnostics, and vector-control preparedness in co-endemic regions.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
Dengue virus (DENV) and chikungunya virus (CHIKV) are Aedes-borne arboviruses with overlapping clinical manifestations, shared vectors, and substantial diagnostic challenges in co-endemic settings.
Evidence base and method
This systematic review and meta-analysis synthesized published evidence on molecular detection, serological positivity, and DENV–CHIKV dual positivity/co-infection in human clinical, surveillance, and community-based study populations. Following PRISMA 2020 guidance, five bibliographic databases (PubMed/MEDLINE, Scopus, Web of Science, ScienceDirect, and Google Scholar) and supplementary grey-literature/preprint sources were searched for English-language studies published from 1 January 1980 to 31 December 2024. Eligible records reported extractable numerators and denominators for DENV and/or CHIKV in humans using recognized molecular or serological assays.
Consensus and conclusions
The pooled DENV estimate was 13.8 % (95 % CI: 10.9–17.3; I 2 =99.0 %), with molecular and serological estimates of 13.1 % (95 % CI: 7.9–21.0) and 14.3 % (95 % CI: 10.2–19.8), respectively. The pooled CHIKV estimate was 14.0 % (95 % CI: 12.0–16.4; I 2 =97.5 %), with molecular and serological estimates of 9.8 and 15.7 %, respectively. DENV–CHIKV dual positivity/co-infection was 52.9 % (95 % CI: 48.7–57.1) among studies that tested and reported both outcomes. Available evidence indicates extensive but highly heterogeneous DENV and CHIKV positivity across selected clinical and surveillance populations.
Controversies and gaps
The pooled estimates should be interpreted cautiously because of substantial between-study heterogeneity, diagnostic variability, outbreak-period sampling, and uneven geographic representation. Country-level estimates varied widely and should be interpreted as summaries of available studies rather than nationally representative burden estimates. Funnel-plot asymmetry was statistically significant in DENV analyses but not in the overall CHIKV analysis. No prospective PROSPERO or OSF protocol registration was available.
Research and practice implications
The findings support integrated arboviral surveillance, multiplex diagnostics, and vector-control preparedness in co-endemic regions.

登革病毒 十年来对墨西哥的经济影响:系统文献综述(2010-2020)

Economic Impact of Dengue Virus in Mexico Over One Decade: A Systematic Literature Review (2010–2020)
作者： Gilberto Sánchez‐González, Luz del C. Hernández‐Hernández, Elaine Gallagher, Gilberto Sánchez-González, Luz Del C Hernández-Hernández
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本综述研究了 2010 年至 2020 年十年间 监测 对墨西哥的经济影响,重点关注与护理、预防和 监测 相关的直接和间接成本。
证据基础与方法
使用 Embase、MEDLINE、Evidence-Based Medicine Reviews、SciELO 和 LILACS 进行了系统文献综述,并对灰色文献和国家卫生部报告进行了额外审查。使用 PICOS 框架和威尔士国家卫生服务质量工具对 2010 年至 2020 年期间以英语或西班牙语发表的研究进行了评估。
共识与结论
The economic burden of dengue in Mexico is substantial, peaking in 2013 with annual costs of $176.82 million (adjusted present value: $281.60 million). All included studies reported nationwide costs, with three focusing on specific Mexican states.
争议与缺口
成本研究之间方法论的异质性挑战了直接比较,并且该审查没有解决除所提到的三个特定州之外的区域差异。
科研与实践启示
需要进一步研究来标准化成本方法并探索区域经济影响。应优化 监测 和 暴发 响应策略,以减轻医疗保健系统成本和生产力损失。

Economic Impact of Dengue Virus in Mexico Over One Decade: A Systematic Literature Review (2010–2020)

Authors: Gilberto Sánchez‐González, Luz del C. Hernández‐Hernández, Elaine Gallagher, Gilberto Sánchez-González, Luz Del C Hernández-Hernández
Original abstract (English)
ABSTRACT Background Dengue is a major mosquito‐borne viral disease worldwide, imposing significant healthcare burden on governments in countries where it is endemic, such as Mexico. This systematic review focused on studies reporting the cost of dengue in Mexico during 2010–2020. To the best of our knowledge, this is the first review discussing this topic in Mexico. We analysed all published direct costs associated with care, prevention, and surveillance, as well as indirect costs. Methods A comprehensive online search was conducted using Embase, MEDLINE, Evidence‐Based Medicine Reviews, SciELO, and LILACS. Articles reporting cost data published during 2010–2020 in English and Spanish were included. Reports from the National Ministry of Health and grey literature during the same period were also reviewed. Following the PICOS (Population, Intervention, Comparison, Outcome and Study design) framework, articles reporting direct and indirect costs were included. The National Health Service Wales tool was used to assess the quality of the studies included. Results Seven articles fulfiled the inclusion criteria. All articles reported the nationwide cost of dengue, and three focused on specific Mexican states. The dengue‐related economic burden was substantial, peaking in 2013, with annual costs of $176.82 million (adjusted present value, $281.60 million). All the searches were verified in January 2023. Conclusions The economic burden of dengue in Mexico is high and is further impacted by outbreaks, resulting in significant costs to the healthcare system and productivity losses. The heterogeneity of the methodological approaches in the cost studies reviewed makes it a challenge to compare them.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This review examines the economic impact of dengue virus in Mexico over the decade 2010–2020, focusing on direct and indirect costs associated with care, prevention, and surveillance.
Evidence base and method
A systematic literature review was conducted using Embase, MEDLINE, Evidence-Based Medicine Reviews, SciELO, and LILACS, with additional review of grey literature and National Ministry of Health reports. Studies published in English or Spanish between 2010–2020 were assessed using the PICOS framework and the National Health Service Wales tool for quality.
Consensus and conclusions
The economic burden of dengue in Mexico is substantial, peaking in 2013 with annual costs of $176.82 million (adjusted present value: $281.60 million). All included studies reported nationwide costs, with three focusing on specific Mexican states.
Controversies and gaps
Heterogeneity in methodological approaches among cost studies challenges direct comparison, and the review does not address regional variations beyond the three specific states mentioned.
Research and practice implications
Further research is needed to standardize cost methodologies and explore regional economic impacts. Surveillance and outbreak response strategies should be optimized to mitigate healthcare system costs and productivity losses.

通过横断面血清调查揭示登革热血清型特异性 传播、交叉反应性和免疫特征

Uncovering dengue serotype-specific transmission, cross-reactivity, and immune profiles from cross-sectional serosurveys
作者： Yining Chen, Angkana T. Huang, Hannah E. Clapham, Huang, A. T., Clapham, H. E.
摘要中文翻译摘要 登革热仍然是一个日益严重的全球健康问题,有四种共同流行的血清型(DENV-1 至 DENV-4)。横断面血清调查对于推断过去的 传播 和群体免疫力至关重要,但血清型特异性解释受到血清型间抗体交叉反应性的限制。在这里,我们开发了一种新颖的建模框架,可以从横断面血清调查中联合推断血清型特异性感染力(FOI)和交叉反应性。该模型对数据粒度非常灵活,可单独使用二元血清状态或与定量滴度结合使用。在模拟中,该模型有效地恢复了不同流行情况、抽样设计和样本年龄覆盖范围的 FOI 和交叉反应性。该模型应用于越南的年度横断面血清调查(2013-2017),估计的血清型特异性 FOI 模式在总体规模上与需要补充纵向感染后抗体测量的分析相当,而这些分析很难收集。我们进一步确定了 DENV-3 传播 强度比之前估计的更高。我们的估计揭示了初次感染后感染血清型和异源抗体反应血清型之间的不对称交叉反应性,以及初次感染后的高交叉反应性。我们根据年龄、时间、血清型和既往感染数量重建了群体免疫特征,解决了与观察到的 血清流行率 无法直接区分的单血清型和多血清型暴露史。我们的估计显示,越南每种血清型的继发感染易感性在大约 10 岁时达到峰值,在 1-30 岁的个体中,DENV-1 至 DENV-3 的平均感染率约为 25%,DENV-4 的平均感染率约为 30%。此外,尽管存在广泛的交叉反应,但合并滴度可以实现个体水平的感染史推断,并在滴度曲线中揭示暴露史信号。这些发现表明,改进的模型可以大大扩展从横截面 血清学 中可恢复的信息,加强血清调查在风险评估、负担估计和 疫苗接种 规划方面的公共卫生效用。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述重点关注 登革病毒(DENV-1 至 DENV-4)及其 传播、交叉反应性和使用横断面血清调查的免疫特征。核心问题是如何从横截面数据推断血清型特异性感染力(FOI)和交叉反应性,解决传统方法的局限性。
证据基础与方法
这是 2026 年发表在 medRxiv 上的一篇综述文章。作者开发了一种新颖的建模框架,可以从横断面血清调查中联合推断血清型特异性 FOI 和交叉反应性。该模型经过了模拟测试,并应用于越南的年度血清调查(2013-2017 年)。
共识与结论
该模型有效地恢复了不同流行病和抽样设计的 FOI 和交叉反应性。应用到越南数据,它估计了与纵向方法相当的血清型特异性 FOI 模式,并确定了更高的 DENV-3 传播 强度。在感染血清型和异源抗体反应血清型之间观察到不对称交叉反应性,在初次感染后具有高交叉反应性。
争议与缺口
该综述没有报告具体的争议,但强调横断面血清调查的血清型特异性解释受到抗体交叉反应性的限制。该模型解决了这一差距,但审查并未讨论数据粒度或越南以外区域适用性的限制。
科研与实践启示
改进的模型可以扩展从横截面 血清学 中恢复的信息,加强风险评估、负担估计和 疫苗接种 规划的公共卫生实用性。尽管存在交叉反应的挑战,该模型仍然能够重建群体免疫特征和个体水平的感染史推断。

Uncovering dengue serotype-specific transmission, cross-reactivity, and immune profiles from cross-sectional serosurveys

Authors: Yining Chen, Angkana T. Huang, Hannah E. Clapham, Huang, A. T., Clapham, H. E.
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Dengue remains a growing global health concern, with four co-circulating serotypes (DENV-1 to DENV-4). Cross-sectional serosurveys are essential for inferring past transmission and population immunity, but serotype-specific interpretation is constrained by antibody cross-reactivity across serotypes. Here, we developed a novel modelling framework to jointly infer serotype-specific force of infection (FOI) and cross-reactivity from cross-sectional serosurveys. The model is flexible to data granularity, functioning with binary serostatus alone or combined with quantitative titres. In simulations, the model effectively recovered FOI and cross-reactivity across varying endemicity, sampling designs, and sample age coverage. Applied to annual cross-sectional serosurveys in Vietnam (2013-2017), the model estimated serotype-specific FOI patterns comparable in overall magnitude to analysis requiring supplementary longitudinal post-infection antibody measurements, which are difficult to collect. We further identified higher DENV-3 transmission intensity than previously estimated. Our estimates revealed asymmetric cross-reactivity between infecting and heterologous antibody-response serotypes after primary infections, and high cross-reactivity after post-primary infections. We reconstructed population immune profiles by age, time, serotype, and past infection number, resolving single-serotype and multi-serotype exposure histories not directly distinguishable from observed seroprevalence. Our estimates showed that susceptibility to secondary infection in Vietnam peaked at approximately age 10 for each serotype, and averaged ∼25% for DENV-1 to DENV-3 and ∼30% for DENV-4 among individuals aged 1-30 years. Additionally, incorporating titres enabled individual-level infection-history inference and revealed exposure-history signals in titre profiles despite extensive cross-reactivity. These findings show that improved modelling can substantially expand the information recoverable from cross-sectional serology, strengthening the public-health utility of serosurveys for risk assessment, burden estimation, and vaccination planning.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on dengue virus (DENV-1 to DENV-4) and its transmission, cross-reactivity, and immune profiles using cross-sectional serosurveys. The central question is how to infer serotype-specific force of infection (FOI) and cross-reactivity from cross-sectional data, addressing limitations in traditional methods.
Evidence base and method
This is a review article published in medRxiv in 2026. The authors developed a novel modeling framework to jointly infer serotype-specific FOI and cross-reactivity from cross-sectional serosurveys. The model was tested in simulations and applied to annual serosurveys in Vietnam (2013-2017).
Consensus and conclusions
The model effectively recovered FOI and cross-reactivity across varying endemicity and sampling designs. Applied to Vietnam data, it estimated serotype-specific FOI patterns comparable to longitudinal methods and identified higher DENV-3 transmission intensity. Asymmetric cross-reactivity was observed between infecting and heterologous antibody-response serotypes, with high cross-reactivity after post-primary infections.
Controversies and gaps
The review does not report specific controversies but highlights that serotype-specific interpretation of cross-sectional serosurveys is constrained by antibody cross-reactivity. The model addresses this gap, but the review does not discuss limitations in data granularity or regional applicability beyond Vietnam.
Research and practice implications
Improved modeling can expand information recoverable from cross-sectional serology, strengthening public-health utility for risk assessment, burden estimation, and vaccination planning. The model enables reconstruction of population immune profiles and individual-level infection-history inference, despite cross-reactivity challenges.

A 高优先级学术文献 · 综述A high priorityAcademic literature · Review

2025 年喀麦隆姆凡德纳 I 卫生区登革热 暴发 调查:伊蚊 媒介 和常规 监测 差距的证据

Dengue outbreak investigation in Mfandena I Health Area, Cameroon, 2025: Evidence of Aedes vectors and routine surveillance gaps
作者： Samantha Awoumou, Vanessa Nzalli, Evaristus Ncham, Flore Balana, Ghislaine Bineli, Olivier Tatsilong, Basile Kamgang, Armel Evouna Mbarga, Armelle Ngomba, Linda Esso
摘要中文翻译简介:2024 年 12 月,喀麦隆雅温得姆凡德纳 I 卫生区 (HA) 报告登革热病例突然增加。通报了两例未按常规 监测 进行的确诊病例,但可用数据很少。部署了一个多学科团队来进行积极的病例发现,确定潜在的 媒介 及其幼虫栖息地,并评估登革热 监测 系统。方法:我们于 2025 年 1 月(旱季)在姆凡德纳 I HA 进行了一项横断面研究。病例是通过医疗记录审查和社区筛查发现的。疑似病例是自2024年11月以来居住在Mfandena I HA的人,发烧>38.5°C,持续2-7天,并且至少有以下两种症状:头痛、眼眶后疼痛、肌痛、关节痛、皮疹、出血表现。对同意的疑似病例及其接触者进行了免疫球蛋白M(IgM)和非结构蛋白1(NS1)检测。对病例住所周围1公里半径范围内进行了昆虫学调查。我们遵循 CDC 框架并结合 WHO IDSR 方法进行 监测 系统评估。结果:我们发现了 46 例登革热病例(44 例疑似病例和 02 例确诊病例)和 16 名接触者,但没有确诊其他病例。登革热病例多为21岁以上女性,以发热、关节痛、头痛为主要症状。确诊病例为成年男性:一名居住在喀麦隆,一名来自法国,两人均出现发烧和红斑皮疹症状。在收集的 143 只伊蚊中,Ae.白纹伊蚊占88%(126/143)。我们检测到一个废弃轮胎含有AE。白纹伊蚊幼虫为可能的感染源。监测 系统在检测和报告方面存在缺陷,既不简单、不可接受,也不稳定。只有 16% (4/25) 的工作人员熟悉病例定义,没有站点有通知表,也没有报告过病例。结论:这项调查表明 Mfandena I HA 中存在潜在的登革热 监测,并强调了限制及时检测和报告的重要 监测 差距。加强监测能力和加强常规昆虫学调查对于改进监测检测、应对和预防至关重要。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该研究的重点是 2025 年在喀麦隆姆凡德纳 I 卫生区进行的 登革病毒 暴发 调查,核心问题是识别伊蚊 媒介 的证据并评估常规 监测 差距。
证据基础与方法
2025 年 1 月(旱季)在姆凡德纳 I 卫生区进行了一项横断面研究。病例是通过医疗记录审查和社区筛查发现的,疑似病例的定义是发烧>38.5°C,持续2-7天,并有特定症状。进行了免疫球蛋白M(IgM)和非结构蛋白1(NS1)检测,并在病例居住地周围1公里半径内进行了昆虫学调查。
共识与结论
调查确认了 46 例登革热病例(44 例疑似病例,2 例确诊病例),并确定白纹伊蚊为主要 媒介(占收集蚊子的 88%)。含有 Ae 的废弃轮胎。白纹伊蚊幼虫被确定为可能的感染源。监测 系统存在显着差距,只有 16% 的员工熟悉案例定义,并且没有可用的通知表格。
争议与缺口
监测 系统被发现既不简单、不可接受、也不稳定,在检测和报告方面存在差距。在接触者中没有确认其他病例,并且该研究仅限于旱季,可能遗漏了 传播 或 媒介 活动的季节性变化。
科研与实践启示
加强监测能力和加强常规昆虫学调查对于改进监测检测、响应和预防至关重要。该研究强调需要对员工进行更好的病例定义培训和改进通知系统。

Dengue outbreak investigation in Mfandena I Health Area, Cameroon, 2025: Evidence of Aedes vectors and routine surveillance gaps

Authors: Samantha Awoumou, Vanessa Nzalli, Evaristus Ncham, Flore Balana, Ghislaine Bineli, Olivier Tatsilong, Basile Kamgang, Armel Evouna Mbarga, Armelle Ngomba, Linda Esso
Original abstract (English)
Introduction: In December 2024, a sudden increase in dengue cases was reported in Mfandena I health area (HA), Yaounde, Cameroon. Two confirmed cases that escaped routine surveillance were notified, and little data were available. A multidisciplinary team was deployed to conduct active case-finding, identify potential vectors and their larval habitats, and evaluate the dengue surveillance system. Methods: We conducted a cross-sectional study in January 2025 (dry season) in Mfandena I HA. Cases were identified through healthcare records reviews and community screening. Suspected cases were people living in Mfandena I HA with fever >38.5°C lasting 2-7 days and at least two of the following symptoms: headache, retro-orbital pain, myalgia, arthralgia, skin rash, hemorrhagic manifestations since November 2024. Immunoglobulin M (IgM) and non-structural protein 1 (NS1) tests were carried out on consenting suspected cases and their contacts. An entomological survey was conducted within a 1 km radius around the case residences. We followed the CDC framework combined with the WHO IDSR approach for surveillance system evaluation. Results: We identified 46 dengue cases (44 suspected cases and 02 confirmed cases) and 16 contacts, but no additional cases were confirmed. Dengue cases were mostly women aged 21 years or older, with fever, arthralgia, and headache as main symptoms. The confirmed cases were male adults: one residing in Cameroon and one arriving from France, both presenting with fever and a red-spot rash. Among 143 Aedes mosquitoes collected, Ae. albopictus accounted for 88% (126/143). We detected a discarded tyre containing Ae. albopictus larvae as the likely source of infection. The surveillance system exhibited gaps in detection and reporting and was neither simple, acceptable, nor stable. Only 16% (4/25) of staff were familiar with the case definition, no sites had notification forms, and no cases had ever been reported. Conclusion: This investigation suggests potential dengue transmission in Mfandena I HA and highlights important surveillance gaps that limited timely detection and reporting. Strengthening surveillance capacity and reinforcing routine entomological investigations are essential to improve outbreak detection, response and prevention.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The study focused on dengue virus outbreak investigation in Mfandena I Health Area, Cameroon, in 2025, with the central question being to identify evidence of Aedes vectors and evaluate routine surveillance gaps.
Evidence base and method
A cross-sectional study was conducted in January 2025 (dry season) in Mfandena I Health Area. Cases were identified through healthcare records reviews and community screening, with suspected cases defined by fever >38.5°C lasting 2-7 days and specific symptoms. Immunoglobulin M (IgM) and non-structural protein 1 (NS1) tests were performed, and an entomological survey was conducted within a 1 km radius around case residences.
Consensus and conclusions
The investigation confirmed 46 dengue cases (44 suspected, 2 confirmed) and identified Aedes albopictus as the primary vector (88% of collected mosquitoes). A discarded tyre containing Ae. albopictus larvae was identified as the likely infection source. The surveillance system exhibited significant gaps, with only 16% of staff familiar with case definitions and no notification forms available.
Controversies and gaps
The surveillance system was found to be neither simple, acceptable, nor stable, with gaps in detection and reporting. No additional cases were confirmed among contacts, and the study was limited to the dry season, potentially missing seasonal variations in transmission or vector activity.
Research and practice implications
Strengthening surveillance capacity and reinforcing routine entomological investigations are essential to improve outbreak detection, response, and prevention. The study highlights the need for better staff training on case definitions and improved notification systems.

全球登革热临床实践指南的方法学质量。系统回顾。

The methodological quality of dengue clinical practice guidelines globally. A systematic review.
作者： Gary K K Low, Sam Froze Jiee, Maria Fernanda Velasquez Leiva, Leticia Del Carmen Castillo Signor, Osamudiamen Favour Omosumwen, Selvanaayagam Shanmuganathan, Maria Lastra Cagigas, Vivian Monzon Gomez, Olaf Horstick, Carlos Alberto Montenegro-Quiñonez
摘要中文翻译目的:本研究旨在系统评价全球登革热临床实践指南(CPG)的方法学质量。方法:本系统评价包括登革热临床管理的 CPG(临床 诊断、调查和 治疗)以及指南的制定过程,要求以证据为基础,并包括专家评审过程。使用 Google、Google Scholar 和 PubMed 进行电子搜索。上次检索是在 2025 年 9 月进行的。没有语言限制。CPG 的选择和提取数据的交叉检查由两名独立评审员进行,第三名独立评审员负责解决任何差异。四位独立评审员使用研究和评估指南评估 II (AGREE-II) 来评估 CPG 的质量。结果:共有 29 个 2009 年至 2025 年在 18 个国家发布的 CPG 被纳入审查。总体而言,有 6 个消费品被评为高质量。在这 6 个 CPG 中,只有 3 个报告了 GRADE 的建议。四个 CPG 在没有透明的审核流程的情况下改编了其他 CPG。大多数 CPG 未能展示出稳健的科学发展过程。结论:尽管本次审查评估的 CPG 质量存在差异,但仍确定了一些适合使用的高质量指南。相比之下,一些 CPG 严重依赖现有指南的改编,同时缺乏足够的方法严谨性。因此,采纳其他来源建议的 CPG 仍应按照 AGREE II 框架进行全面、系统的开发过程。

共识与结论
尽管本次审查评估的 CPG 质量存在差异,但仍确定了一些适合使用的高质量指南。在这 6 个 CPG 中,只有 3 个报告了 GRADE 的建议。大多数 CPG 未能展示出稳健的科学发展过程。
争议与缺口
四个 CPG 在没有透明的审核流程的情况下改编了其他 CPG。相比之下,一些 CPG 严重依赖现有指南的改编,同时缺乏足够的方法严谨性。
科研与实践启示

The methodological quality of dengue clinical practice guidelines globally. A systematic review.

Authors: Gary K K Low, Sam Froze Jiee, Maria Fernanda Velasquez Leiva, Leticia Del Carmen Castillo Signor, Osamudiamen Favour Omosumwen, Selvanaayagam Shanmuganathan, Maria Lastra Cagigas, Vivian Monzon Gomez, Olaf Horstick, Carlos Alberto Montenegro-Quiñonez
Original abstract (English)
OBJECTIVE: This study aimed to systematically review the methodological quality of dengue clinical practice guidelines (CPGs) available globally. METHODS: This systematic review included CPGs on dengue clinical management (clinical diagnosis, investigation and treatment), and the development process of the guidelines, required to be evidence-based and including an expert review process. An electronic search was performed using Google, Google Scholar and PubMed. The search was last performed in September 2025. No language restriction was imposed. The selection of CPGs and cross-checking of data extracted were performed by two independent reviewers with a third to resolve any discrepancies. The Appraisal of Guidelines for Research and Evaluation II (AGREE-II) was used to assess the quality of CPGs by four independent reviewers. RESULTS: A total of 29 CPGs published from 2009-2025 in 18 countries were included for review. Overall, six CPGs rated as high quality. Among these six CPGs, only three reported the recommendations with GRADE. Four CPGs adapted other CPGs without transparent review process. Majority of the CPGs fail to demonstrate a robust scientific development process. CONCLUSION: Despite the variability in the quality of the CPGs assessed in this review, several high-quality guidelines were identified as suitable for use. In contrast, a number of CPGs were found to rely heavily on adaptations of existing guidelines while lacking sufficient methodological rigour. Therefore, CPGs that adapt recommendations from other sources should still undertake a full and systematic development process consistent with the AGREE II framework.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This study aimed to systematically review the methodological quality of dengue clinical practice guidelines (CPGs) available globally.
Evidence base and method
This systematic review included CPGs on dengue clinical management (clinical diagnosis, investigation and treatment), and the development process of the guidelines, required to be evidence-based and including an expert review process. An electronic search was performed using Google, Google Scholar and PubMed. The search was last performed in September 2025. No language restriction was imposed. The selection of CPGs and cross-checking of data extracted were performed by two independent reviewers with a third to resolve any discrepancies. The Appraisal of Guidelines for Research and Evaluation II (AGREE-II) was used to assess the quality of CPGs by four independent reviewers.
Consensus and conclusions
Despite the variability in the quality of the CPGs assessed in this review, several high-quality guidelines were identified as suitable for use. Among these six CPGs, only three reported the recommendations with GRADE. Majority of the CPGs fail to demonstrate a robust scientific development process.
Controversies and gaps
Four CPGs adapted other CPGs without transparent review process. In contrast, a number of CPGs were found to rely heavily on adaptations of existing guidelines while lacking sufficient methodological rigour.
Research and practice implications
Therefore, CPGs that adapt recommendations from other sources should still undertake a full and systematic development process consistent with the AGREE II framework.

Métodos eficazes de prevenção à dengue 预防登革热的有效方法

Métodos eficazes de prevenção à dengue Effective methods for dengue prevention
作者： Romeu Hartwig
摘要中文翻译登革热是一种虫媒病毒性疾病,主要由埃及伊蚊传播,是巴西的一个主要公共卫生问题。近年来病例的大幅增加凸显了加强疾病预防和控制策略的必要性。本研究旨在通过回顾科学文献和卫生部的官方文件来确定预防登革热的有效方法。该研究的特点是定性、描述性和参考书目。结果表明,消除繁殖地、健康教育、社区参与、流行病学监测以及媒介控制剂的持续行动是减少媒介扩散的基本措施。结论是,解决登革热问题取决于公共当局和民众的整合,预防是减少该疾病的 发病率 及其社会和经济影响的最有效策略。

Métodos eficazes de prevenção à dengue Effective methods for dengue prevention

Authors: Romeu Hartwig
Original abstract (English)
Dengue is an arboviral disease transmitted mainly by the Aedes aegypti mosquito and represents a major public health problem in Brazil. The significant increase in cases in recent years highlights the need to strengthen disease prevention and control strategies. This study aims to identify effective methods for dengue prevention through a review of the scientific literature and official documents from the Ministry of Health. The research is characterized as qualitative, descriptive, and bibliographic. The results demonstrate that the elimination of breeding sites, health education, community participation, epidemiological surveillance, and continuous actions by vector-control agents are fundamental measures for reducing vector proliferation. It is concluded that addressing dengue depends on the integration of public authorities and the population, with prevention being the most effective strategy for reducing the incidence of the disease and its social and economic impacts.

🚨 突发动态与新闻 / Health News

Published: 2026-07-27  |  Publisher: ReliefWeb
A 高优先级公共卫生新闻

太平洋地区的登革热:多国情况(截至 2026 年 7 月 27 日) - ReliefWeb

Dengue in the Pacific: Multicountry Situation (As of 27 July 2026) - ReliefWeb
新闻简报自 2025 年 1 月以来太平洋地区发生的多国登革热 暴发 已导致太平洋岛国和地区确诊 21,258 例病例,21 人死亡。仅 2026 年,就报告了 4,466 例病例、173 例住院治疗和 3 例死亡,其中库克群岛、法属波利尼西亚、萨摩亚和新喀里多尼亚占病例的 94%。新喀里多尼亚、汤加和谢法省(瓦努阿图)正式宣布 暴发,而库克群岛于 5 月结束了 暴发。DENV-1和DENV-2正在传播,谢法省正在流行传播,其他地区的水平较低。太平洋综合症 监测 系统注意到,与前一年相比,登革热类疾病病例呈下降趋势。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布日期:2026年7月27日。活动期限:自2025年1月1日起,更新至2026年7月27日。
地点与对象
太平洋岛屿国家和地区 (PIC),包括美属萨摩亚、库克群岛、斐济、法属波利尼西亚、基里巴斯、瑙鲁、新喀里多尼亚、萨摩亚、谢法省(瓦努阿图)、汤加、图瓦卢以及瓦利斯和富图纳。
事件
自 2025 年 1 月 1 日以来,多个太平洋岛屿国家和地区确诊登革热 暴发,瓦努阿图谢法省正在发生 暴发,截至 2026 年 7 月 27 日,其他地区的感染率较低。
规模、影响与风险
自 2025 年 1 月 1 日以来,太平洋地区总共报告了 21,258 例确诊病例和 21 例死亡。仅 2026 年,就记录了 4,466 例确诊病例、173 例住院治疗和 3 例死亡。2026年受影响最大的国家包括库克群岛、法属波利尼西亚、萨摩亚和新喀里多尼亚,占病例数的94%。
应对、状态与不确定性
新喀里多尼亚、汤加和谢法省(瓦努阿图)于2026年正式宣布暴发。库克群岛于2026年5月25日宣布结束其暴发。太平洋综合症监测系统(PSSS)迄今报告了8,723例登革热样疾病(DLI)病例,与去年同期相比呈下降趋势。瓦利斯和富图纳群岛正在根据社区 传播 报告受到监控。
A high priorityPublic-health news

Dengue in the Pacific: Multicountry Situation (As of 27 July 2026) - ReliefWeb

News Brief
A multicountry dengue outbreak in the Pacific since January 2025 has resulted in 21,258 confirmed cases and 21 deaths across Pacific Island Countries and Areas. In 2026 alone, 4,466 cases, 173 hospitalizations, and 3 deaths were reported, with Cook Islands, French Polynesia, Samoa, and New Caledonia accounting for 94% of cases. Outbreaks were officially declared in New Caledonia, Tonga, and Shefa Province (Vanuatu), while the Cook Islands ended its outbreak in May. DENV-1 and DENV-2 are circulating, with ongoing transmission in Shefa Province and low levels in other regions. The Pacific Syndromic Surveillance System notes a lower trend in Dengue-like Illness cases compared to the previous year.

为什么 12,000 卢比的 疫苗 不是印度对抗登革热的灵丹妙药 - 印度快报

Why a Rs 12,000 vaccine isn’t the silver bullet against dengue in India - The Indian Express
新闻简报India has approved Qdenga, its first dengue vaccine, marking a significant step in the fight against the disease. However, the vaccine's high cost of Rs 6,000-12,000 for a two-dose course may limit accessibility, with initial demand expected primarily from the urban middle class. While clinical trials show Qdenga provides long-lasting protection against all four dengue virus types, it is not recommended for children under six, pregnant women, or immunocompromised individuals. India recorded over 2.89 lakh dengue cases and 485 deaths in 2023, with cases already rising in 2026. The vaccine should complement, not replace, comprehensive mosquito control measures, including eliminating breeding sites and community participation. Further epidemiological data is needed to determine which regions qualify for wider public funding.

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Why a Rs 12,000 vaccine isn’t the silver bullet against dengue in India - The Indian Express

News Brief
India has approved Qdenga, its first dengue vaccine, marking a significant step in the fight against the disease. However, the vaccine's high cost of Rs 6,000-12,000 for a two-dose course may limit accessibility, with initial demand expected primarily from the urban middle class. While clinical trials show Qdenga provides long-lasting protection against all four dengue virus types, it is not recommended for children under six, pregnant women, or immunocompromised individuals. India recorded over 2.89 lakh dengue cases and 485 deaths in 2023, with cases already rising in 2026. The vaccine should complement, not replace, comprehensive mosquito control measures, including eliminating breeding sites and community participation. Further epidemiological data is needed to determine which regions qualify for wider public funding.
Original source text (English)
Opinion Why a Rs 12,000 vaccine isn’t the silver bullet against dengue in India Clinical trials have shown that Qdenga provides long-lasting protection against all four dengue virus types in people who have had the disease. It has also been shown to protect against DENV-1 and DENV-2 among people who have never had dengue The same Aedes mosquitoes also transmit chikungunya and Zika, making integrated vector management indispensable. Eliminating mosquito breeding sites through better drainage and waste disposal must remain the first line of defence. . (Source: Getty Images/Thinkstock) Written by: Rajib Dasgupta 3 min read Jul 30, 2026 12:49 AM IST First published on: Jul 29, 2026 at 05:32 PM IST India has taken an important step in the fight against dengue. On July 21, the Central Drugs Standard Control Organisation granted marketing authorisation to Qdenga , the country’s first approved dengue vaccine. Manufactured by Takeda GmbH, Germany, and marketed in India by Takeda Biopharmaceuticals, the vaccine has already been approved in 42 countries, including EU members, Indonesia, Thailand, Malaysia, Argentina and Brazil. More than 24 million doses have been administered worldwide, and the vaccine has the WHO’s imprimatur. Dengue, one of the world’s fastest-spreading mosquito-borne viral diseases, is caused by four closely related virus types — DENV-1, DENV-2, DENV-3 and DENV-4 — and is transmitted primarily by the Aedes aegypti mosquito. Clinical trials have shown that Qdenga provides long-lasting protection against all four dengue virus types in people who have had the disease. It has also been shown to protect against DENV-1 and DENV-2 among people who have never had dengue, with particularly strong protection against DENV-2, on which the vaccine is based. Advertisement The vaccine is administered in two doses, three months apart. If the second dose is delayed, the vaccination schedule does not need to be restarted; the missed dose can be given at the earliest opportunity. The vaccine, however, is not recommended for children below six years because of lower efficacy and the relatively low prevalence of prior dengue infection in that age group. The WHO also does not recommend its use beyond 60 years until there is more evidence on safety and efficacy. It should not be administered to pregnant women, women planning pregnancy within one month of vaccination, breastfeeding mothers, immunocompromised individuals, or those receiving immunosuppressive therapies such as chemotherapy or high-dose corticosteroids. Affordability is likely to determine uptake. At an expected price of Rs 3,000-6,000 per dose, the two-dose course could cost Rs 6,000-12,000. Initial demand is, therefore, likely to come primarily from the urban middle class. Whether the vaccine should eventually be included in the Universal Immunisation Programme (UIP) is a complex question. The WHO recommends that countries consider introducing Qdenga into routine immunisation only in areas where dengue transmission is a significant public health threat. Indicators include a dengue seroprevalence exceeding 60 per cent by the age of nine years or a mean age of dengue-related hospitalisation below 16 years. India will need robust epidemiological data to determine which regions meet these criteria before considering wider public funding. Advertisement India recorded more than 2.89 lakh dengue cases and 485 deaths in 2023. Around 7,000 cases and 10 deaths have already been reported this year, with the annual surge expected during the next two or three months. you may like 1 Shashi Tharoor writes: Why an 850-seat Lok Sabha threatens Indian democracy 2 BJP’s missing diagnosis: Why couldn’t the Sangh Parivar hear the young? 3 Shashi Tharoor writes: Why an 850-seat Lok Sabha threatens Indian democracy 4 Tarique Rahman’s first foreign tour bypasses India. It’s a reset, not a rupture 5 Demolition of Mohammad Ali Jauhar University: On campus, questions about vendetta politics Vaccination, however, is only one part of the solution. The same Aedes mosquitoes also transmit chikungunya and Zika, making integrated vector management indispensable. Eliminating mosquito breeding sites through better drainage and waste disposal must remain the first line of defence. These efforts should be complemented by biological measures such as larvivorous fish, judicious use of chemical and microbial control methods, stronger coordination across government agencies, and sustained community participation. Improving public awareness and health literacy is equally important. Qdenga is a significant addition to India’s public health arsenal, but it should complement, not replace, a comprehensive strategy for controlling mosquito-borne diseases. The writer is chairperson, Centre of Social Medicine and Community Health, JNU © The Indian Express Pvt Ltd # Delhi dengue # dengue # vaccine Latest Comment Post Comment Read Comments

为什么印度的 登革病毒 组合变得更加危险? |解释

Why is India’s dengue virus mix becoming more dangerous? | Explained
新闻简报印度医学研究委员会 (ICMR) 病毒研究和诊断实验室 (VRDL) 进行的一项为期两年的全国性 监测 研究显示,印度的登革热疫情正在进入一个更加复杂的阶段,多种病毒株目前在该国大部分地区同时传播。这种模式被称为高流行性 传播,它会增加患严重疾病的风险,使 暴发 更难以预测,并使未来的 疫苗接种 策略变得复杂。该研究题为“2023-2025 年登革热血清型的泛印度分子 监测”,清晰地描述了登革热在印度的演变情况,并强调了这些发现对于 暴发 管理、严重疾病预防和 疫苗接种 政策的重要性。班加罗尔等受影响地区正在开展灭蚊活动。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布日期:2026年7月29日。活动期限:2023年至2025年进行的为期两年的全国监测研究。
地点与对象
受影响人口:全国大部分地区的一般人口,特别提到班加罗尔。
事件
已确认的流行病学转变:印度正在进入一个新阶段,多种 登革病毒 毒株(DENV-1、DENV-2、DENV-3、DENV-4)同时传播,称为高流行 传播。报告者:印度医学研究委员会 (ICMR) 病毒研究和诊断实验室 (VRDL)。
规模、影响与风险
案例:证据中未具体说明。风险:严重疾病的风险增加,暴发 预测更加困难,以及由于 传播 的高流行而使未来的 疫苗接种 策略变得复杂。
应对、状态与不确定性
调查:正在进行分子 监测 研究。回应:提到班加罗尔的灭蚊活动。不确定性:未来的疫苗接种策略很复杂;高流行性 传播 的长期影响仍在研究中。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Why is India’s dengue virus mix becoming more dangerous? | Explained

News Brief
India's dengue epidemic is entering a more complex phase, with multiple virus strains now circulating simultaneously across much of the country, according to a two-year nationwide surveillance study by the Indian Council of Medical Research's (ICMR) Virus Research and Diagnostic Laboratory (VRDL). This pattern, known as hyperendemic transmission, increases the risk of severe illness, makes outbreaks harder to predict, and complicates future vaccination strategies. The study, titled 'Pan-India Molecular Surveillance of Dengue Serotypes, 2023–2025,' provides a clear picture of how dengue is evolving in India and highlights the importance of these findings for outbreak management, severe disease prevention, and vaccination policy. Mosquito control activities are being implemented in affected areas like Bengaluru.
Original source text (English)

印度首例登革热 疫苗 抵达,并附带重要的安全警告 - 《印度教徒报》

India’s first dengue vaccine arrives with a crucial safety caveat - The Hindu
新闻简报印度已批准其首个登革热 疫苗 Qdenga,该药物针对所有四种 登革病毒 血清型。虽然 疫苗 提供广泛的保护,但临床试验揭示了一个关键的安全问题:它可能会增强以前没有接触过的儿童的登革热感染。在这种情况下,疫苗 可以模拟原发感染,防止 DENV-1 和 DENV-2,但使接收者容易受到 DENV-3 的感染。Qdenga 已在 40 多个国家/地区获得批准,在印度的推出凸显了仔细筛查的必要性,以确保仅对之前接触过登革热的个人进行治疗。疫苗 的功效和安全性仍然密切相关,需要持续的 监测 来减轻潜在风险。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026 年 7 月 27 日,更新于 2026 年 7 月 29 日。
地点与对象
印度;幼儿和4-60岁的成人。
事件
印度药品监管机构批准了该国首个登革热 疫苗 Qdenga,旨在预防所有四种 登革病毒 血清型(DENV-1 至 DENV-4)。然而,临床试验数据引起了人们对登革热儿童中潜在的 抗体依赖性增强 的担忧。
规模、影响与风险
Qdenga 已在 40 多个国家获得批准。疫苗 可能会增强儿童的登革热感染,而无需事先接触,可能提供针对 DENV-1 和 DENV-2 的保护,但不能针对 DENV-3。
应对、状态与不确定性
此次批准基于 2019 年、2020 年和 2024 年的 3 期试验数据。疫苗 的有效性和安全性是相关的,需要进一步监测以解决登革热初次感染者的抗体增强风险。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

India’s first dengue vaccine arrives with a crucial safety caveat - The Hindu

News Brief
India has approved its first dengue vaccine, Qdenga, which targets all four dengue virus serotypes. While the vaccine offers broad protection, clinical trials reveal a critical safety concern: it may enhance dengue infection in children with no prior exposure. In such cases, the vaccine could simulate a primary infection, protecting against DENV-1 and DENV-2 but leaving recipients vulnerable to DENV-3. Approved in over 40 countries, Qdenga's rollout in India highlights the need for careful screening to ensure it is administered only to individuals with prior dengue exposure. The vaccine's efficacy and safety remain closely linked, requiring ongoing surveillance to mitigate potential risks.
Original source text (English)

Published on July 27, 2026, with an update on July 29, 2026.
Location and population
India; young children and adults aged 4-60 years.
Event
India's drug regulator approved the country's first dengue vaccine, Qdenga, which is designed to protect against all four dengue virus serotypes (DENV-1 to DENV-4). However, clinical trial data raise concerns about potential antibody-dependent enhancement in dengue-naive children.
Scale, impact and risk
Qdenga has been approved in over 40 countries. The vaccine may enhance dengue infection in children without prior exposure, potentially offering protection against DENV-1 and DENV-2 but not DENV-3.

The approval is based on phase-3 trial data from 2019, 2020, and 2024. The efficacy and safety of the vaccine are linked, and further monitoring is needed to address the antibody enhancement risk in dengue-naive individuals.

印度批准首个登革热 疫苗 Qdenga 用于 4-60 岁人群 - Daijiworld

India approves first dengue vaccine Qdenga for people aged 4-60 - Daijiworld
新闻简报印度已批准首个登革热 疫苗(Qdenga)用于 4 至 60 岁的人群,这标志着对抗蚊媒疾病的一个重要里程碑。中央药品标准控制组织 (CDSCO) 授予了活减毒四价 疫苗 的营销授权,该产品可预防所有四种 登革病毒 血清型。Qdenga 采用重组 DNA 技术开发,分两次皮下注射,间隔三个月。此次批准基于在包括印度在内的多个国家进行的广泛临床研究,疫苗 已在 42 个国家获得批准,并通过了 WHO 资格预审。虽然 疫苗 显着降低了登革热和严重疾病的风险,但专家强调,它应该补充而不是取代蚊子控制措施。公共卫生专家强调消除蚊子滋生地、使用驱虫剂以及针对高烧和严重头痛等症状就医的重要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026 年 7 月 25 日;证据中未指定事件日期。
地点与对象
印度;受影响人群包括4-60岁人群,尤其是登革热高发地区暴发。
事件
印度已批准首个登革热 疫苗(Qdenga)用于 4-60 岁的个体。该批准由中央药品标准控制组织 (CDSCO) 批准,标志着抗击登革热的一个重要里程碑,登革热是印度的一项重大公共卫生挑战,特别是在季风季节和季风后季节。
规模、影响与风险
登革热给印度带来了重大的公共卫生挑战,每年季风季节都有数千人住院。Qdenga 旨在预防所有四种 登革病毒 血清型(DENV-1、DENV-2、DENV-3 和 DENV-4)。疫苗 不能保证完全保护,但可以显着降低登革热和严重疾病的风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
The approval is based on extensive clinical studies conducted in multiple countries, including India. Qdenga has been approved in 42 countries and received WHO prequalification. Post-marketing surveillance indicates a favorable safety profile, with over 24 million doses distributed globally. Experts emphasize that vaccination should complement, not replace, mosquito control measures. The vaccine is generally not recommended for people with severe immune suppression or pregnant women unless future guidelines advise otherwise.
B routine priorityPublic-health news

India approves first dengue vaccine Qdenga for people aged 4-60 - Daijiworld

News Brief
India has approved its first dengue vaccine, Qdenga, for individuals aged 4-60 years, marking a significant milestone in combating the mosquito-borne disease. The Central Drugs Standard Control Organisation (CDSCO) granted marketing authorization for the live, attenuated tetravalent vaccine, which protects against all four dengue virus serotypes. Developed using recombinant DNA technology, Qdenga is administered as a subcutaneous injection in two doses, three months apart. The approval is based on extensive clinical studies conducted in multiple countries, including India, and the vaccine has already been approved in 42 countries with WHO prequalification. While the vaccine significantly reduces the risk of dengue and severe disease, experts stress that it should complement, not replace, mosquito control measures. Public health specialists emphasize the importance of eliminating mosquito breeding sites, using repellents, and seeking medical attention for symptoms like high fever and severe headache.
Original source text (English)
Home News Obituary Recipes Charity Special ಕನ್ನಡ Live TV RADIO Advertise Contact Us Matrimonial Properties Jobs General Ads Red Chillies Music Ask Dr Greetings Astrology Tribute Of Love Latest India poised to gain from global biopharma patent expiry boom Wed, Jul 29 Brain changes may be linked to sleep disorders, new study suggests Wed, Jul 29 Monsoon health: Vitamin C-rich foods can help strengthen immunity during rainy season Wed, Jul 29 Study finds heart has its own neural network that helps regulate heartbeat Wed, Jul 29 Uganda declares end of Ebola outbreak Tue, Jul 28 HPV vaccine can help eliminate cervical cancer in India: Dattatreyudu Nori Tue, Jul 28 Weekend binge drinking can silently damage liver, warn doctors Tue, Jul 28 Health India approves first dengue vaccine Qdenga for people aged 4-60 Sat, Jul 25 2026 01:09:51 PM Discover moreFilm AwardsIndian cultural goodsNews portal access Daijiworld Media Network - New Delhi New Delhi, Jul 25: India has approved its first dengue vaccine, Qdenga, marking a major milestone in the country's efforts to combat one of its most widespread mosquito-borne diseases. The Central Drugs Standard Control Organisation (CDSCO) has granted marketing authorisation for Qdenga, a live, attenuated tetravalent dengue vaccine designed to protect against all four dengue virus serotypes—DENV-1, DENV-2, DENV-3 and DENV-4. The approval comes as dengue continues to pose a major public health challenge in India, particularly during the monsoon and post-monsoon seasons, when thousands of people are hospitalised every year. Developed using recombinant DNA technology, Qdenga is supplied as a freeze-dried powder that is reconstituted before administration and is given as a subcutaneous injection. According to the CDSCO approval, the vaccine is indicated for individuals aged between 4 and 60 years. It is administered in two doses of 0.5 ml each, with the second dose given three months after the first. Medical experts said one of the vaccine's key advantages is that recipients do not require routine testing to determine whether they have previously been infected with dengue before vaccination. Dr Dinesh Kumar, Director of Internal Medicine at Fortis Greater Noida, described the approval as an important development in dengue prevention. He said the vaccine offers protection against all four dengue virus serotypes and could particularly benefit people living in regions with frequent dengue outbreaks. However, he noted that vaccination decisions should be made after consultation with a healthcare professional, especially for individuals with underlying medical conditions. As Qdenga is a live-attenuated vaccine, it is generally not recommended for people with severe immune suppression or pregnant women unless future official guidelines advise otherwise. The vaccine's approval is based on extensive clinical studies evaluating its safety, immune response and effectiveness in both dengue-endemic and non-endemic countries. Trials involved participants from more than eight countries, including Brazil, Thailand and Sri Lanka, while a Phase III study was also conducted in India among people aged 4 to 60 years. Qdenga has already been approved in 42 countries, including the European Union, the United Kingdom, Switzerland, Indonesia, Malaysia and Thailand. It has also received World Health Organization (WHO) prequalification, enabling procurement by international health agencies. More than 24 million doses have been distributed globally, with post-marketing surveillance indicating a favourable safety profile. Experts emphasised that while the vaccine significantly reduces the risk of dengue and severe disease, it does not guarantee complete protection against infection. Dr Vikas Maurya, Senior Director and Head of Respiratory Medicine at Fortis Hospital, Shalimar Bagh, said dengue can range from an asymptomatic infection to life-threatening dengue haemorrhagic fever. He stressed that eliminating mosquito breeding sites remains the most effective preventive measure, while vaccination provides an additional layer of protection. Public health specialists have also reiterated that vaccination should complement, rather than replace, mosquito control measures. Preventing stagnant water accumulation, using mosquito repellents, installing window screens, wearing full-sleeved clothing, using mosquito nets and supporting local vector-control programmes remain essential in reducing dengue transmission. Dr Avi Kumar, Senior Consultant in Pulmonology at Fortis Escorts Heart Institute, said the approval of Qdenga represents an important advance in dengue prevention but should form part of a broader strategy that includes mosquito control, environmental sanitation, early diagnosis and timely medical care. Doctors also advised people to consult qualified healthcare professionals before vaccination to assess their eligibility based on age, medical history, immune status and previous dengue exposure. They stressed that anyone experiencing symptoms such as high fever, severe headache, pain behind the eyes, muscle and joint pain, nausea, vomiting, skin rash or unusual bleeding should seek immediate medical attention. Health experts said the approval of India's first dengue vaccine marks a significant scientific achievement and provides an important new tool in the country's fight against dengue, while underscoring that vaccination alone cannot eliminate the disease. 希尔斯伯勒县报告蚊媒登革热病例

Hillsborough County reports case of mosquito-borne dengue
新闻简报据佛罗里达州卫生部称,佛罗里达州希尔斯伯勒县今年夏天报告了首例本地感染的登革热病例。由埃及伊蚊和白纹伊蚊传播的登革热在佛罗里达州很常见,今年全州记录了 42 例病例,去年记录了 506 例。去年,迈阿密戴德县的病例数占 60% 以上,希尔斯伯勒县报告了 28 例。症状包括发烧、严重头痛和关节痛。卫生部门在灭蚊的同时,还进行了空中喷洒,并敦促居民消除蚊子孳生地、使用驱虫剂,如果出现症状就立即就医。除了休息和液体之外,不存在特定的 治疗。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Hillsborough County reports case of mosquito-borne dengue

News Brief
Hillsborough County, Florida, has reported its first locally acquired dengue case this summer, according to the Florida Department of Health. Dengue, transmitted by Aedes aegypti and Aedes albopictus mosquitoes, is common in Florida, with 42 cases recorded statewide this year and 506 last year. Miami-Dade County led with over 60% of cases last year, while Hillsborough reported 28. Symptoms include fever, severe headache, and joint pain. The health department, alongside mosquito control, conducted aerial spraying and urged residents to eliminate mosquito breeding sites, use repellent, and seek medical care if symptoms develop. No specific treatment exists beyond rest and fluids.
Original source text (English)
The Florida Department of Health recorded the first case of dengue acquired locally in Hillsborough County this summer. Dengue fever is a mosquito-borne disease transmitted through the bite of the mosquito species aedes aegypti and aedes albopictus, which are both found in Florida. Most cases of dengue in the U.S. occur in travelers infected in areas with risk , like the Caribbean and Central and South America. In Florida, local spread is common. Typical symptoms of dengue include fever, severe headache, eye pain, muscle, joint and bone pain, bleeding, nausea, vomiting and rash, according to the health department. The joint and bone pain that some people experience gives dengue its second name, bone-break fever. Symptoms typically last 2-7 days and develop within two weeks of an infected mosquito bite. If symptoms of dengue develop, the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention advise seeing a healthcare provider. There is no specific medicine to treat dengue — just rest, drinking fluids and easing symptoms with pain relief. Forty-two cases of dengue have been recorded in Florida so far this year, according to the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention. The center reported 506 cases in Florida last year. Miami-Dade County accounted for over 60% of the state's cases last year. Compared to Miami-Dade's 316 cases, Hillsborough County recorded 28 and Pinellas County had eight in 2025. Hillsborough reported seven locally acquired dengue cases between July 2024 and January 2025. The health departmentand Hillsborough County Mosquito Control coordinated aerial spraying as a prevention effort. Florida is currently in the middle of mosquito season, which runs from March to October. To help prevent mosquito bites, the health department encourages residents to drain water from outside areas to reduce where mosquitoes can breed and lay eggs, cover themselves with protective clothing while outdoors and keep doors and windows closed. The department also recommends applying insect repellent that contains DEET (10-30%), picaridin, oil of lemon eucalyptus, para-menthane-diol, 2-undecanone or IR3535. For children under two months old, the department recommends mosquito netting for protection. Residents should report dead birds to the Florida Fish and Wildlife Conservation Commission's website , according to the department. The Florida Department of Health regularly conducts statewide surveillance for mosquito-borne illnesses, including dengue, West Nile virus infections, Eastern equine encephalitis, St. Louis encephalitis, malaria and chikungunya. 随着南亚病例激增,印度批准首个登革热 疫苗 - aol.ca

India approves first dengue vaccine as cases surge in South Asia - aol.ca
新闻简报India has approved its first dengue vaccine, QDENGA, as cases surge across South Asia. The vaccine, developed by Takeda Pharmaceutical, was approved by India’s CDSCO after trials involving over 20,000 participants. Sri Lanka has reported 76,044 cases and 53 deaths this year, while Bangladesh faces over 9,000 cases and 30 deaths. India accounts for nearly one-third of the global dengue burden. The vaccine, approved for ages 4-60, will be manufactured locally with Biological E, aiming for 50 million annual doses. Post-marketing studies are required, and experts emphasize it must complement vector control and surveillance efforts.
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布日期:2026年7月25日。活动日期:2026年7月20日(批准公告)。
地点与对象
印度(主要),斯里兰卡和孟加拉国也报告了 暴发。受影响人群包括印度4-60岁的成人和儿童。
事件
随着南亚病例激增,印度已批准首个登革热 疫苗(武田制药 QDENGA)。印度药品监管机构 (CDSCO) 根据 III 期试验数据确认了这一批准。
规模、影响与风险
2026年迄今为止,斯里兰卡报告了76,044例病例和53例死亡,单日新增病例2,652例。孟加拉国报告了 9,000 多例病例和 30 人死亡。印度几乎占全球登革热负担的三分之一。这种疾病给卫生系统带来了巨大压力。
应对、状态与不确定性
疫苗 已批准用于成人和 4-60 岁儿童,并通过了 WHO 资格预审。上市后安全性和有效性研究需要在商业上市后六个月内进行。疫苗 补充了现有的 媒介 控制措施,但对孕妇、新生儿或老年人没有好处。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

India approves first dengue vaccine as cases surge in South Asia - aol.ca

News Brief
India has approved its first dengue vaccine, QDENGA, as cases surge across South Asia. The vaccine, developed by Takeda Pharmaceutical, was approved by India’s CDSCO after trials involving over 20,000 participants. Sri Lanka has reported 76,044 cases and 53 deaths this year, while Bangladesh faces over 9,000 cases and 30 deaths. India accounts for nearly one-third of the global dengue burden. The vaccine, approved for ages 4-60, will be manufactured locally with Biological E, aiming for 50 million annual doses. Post-marketing studies are required, and experts emphasize it must complement vector control and surveillance efforts.
Original source text (English)
Samaan Lateef Mon, July 20, 2026 at 4:44 p.m. UTC 0 A municipal worker fumigates a market area during a dengue prevention campaign in Colombo, Sri Lanka - ISHARA S. KODIKARA/AFP via Getty Images India has approved its first dengue vaccine as cases of the mosquito-borne disease surge in South Asia. Japan’s Takeda Pharmaceutical’s dengue vaccine QDENGA has been approved by India’s drug regulator, the Centre Drugs Standard Control Organisation (CDSCO). The global threat posed by dengue has been rising for years, as climate change, urbanisation and international travel aid the spread of the mosquitoes that transmit it. The approval comes as hospitals across South Asia grapple with a surge in dengue infections. In Sri Lanka, health authorities reported 2,652 new cases on Sunday alone, taking the total to 76,044 dengue cases so far this year. At least 53 people have died. Bangladesh is also suffering a major outbreak with over 9,000 cases and 30 deaths that has piled pressure on its health system even as it battles a measles epidemic . Dengue is transmitted to humans through the bites of infected Aedes mosquitoes. Its symptoms include fever, severe muscle pain, and in severe cases, shock and internal bleeding that can be fatal. Although mortality is relatively low, outbreaks place a substantial burden on health systems. Takeda’s QDENGA jab is a live-attenuated tetravalent vaccine designed to protect against all four dengue virus serotypes. It can be given regardless of previous dengue infections. “The approval comes at a critical time for public health, as reported dengue cases in India have increased 11-fold in the past two decades,” the company said in a statement. Advertisement Dengue patients receive treatment at the District General Hospital in Sri Lanka’s Negombo city - ISHARA S. KODIKARA/AFP via Getty Images Takeda estimates that India accounts for almost one-third of the global dengue burden. The approval follows a review of global and Indian phase III trial data involving more than 20,000 participants across eight countries, including Sri Lanka, as well as 480 volunteers in India. Regulators concluded the vaccine was safe and immunogenic in adults and children aged between four and 60 years. However, the CDSCO directed the company to undertake post-marketing safety and effectiveness studies in India within six months of the vaccine’s commercial launch. QDENGA has already been approved in more than 40 countries and is WHO pre-qualified. In 2024, Takeda partnered with Indian vaccine manufacturer Biological E to manufacture up to 50 million doses annually, supporting Takeda’s target of producing 100 million doses globally each year by the end of the decade. Dr Eng Eong, a dengue expert and professor in Emerging Infectious Diseases at Duke-NUS in Singapore, told the Telegraph that it was critical for India and other dengue-affected countries to move beyond strategies reliant on vector-control. “A licenced dengue vaccine [in India] adds an important piece to the very limited public health arsenal to combat dengue,” he said. Shikha Taneja Malik, Senior Scientific Affairs Manager, Drugs for Neglected Diseases initiative, South Asia, said: “A locally manufactured, affordable vaccine could meaningfully reduce severe cases and hospital strain. But there are still specific populations such as pregnant women, newborns, and older adults who would not benefit from this vaccine.” Ms Malik said the vaccination should complement surveillance, vector control and development of new treatments to help reduce the burden of the disease. Location and population
India (primary), with outbreaks also reported in Sri Lanka and Bangladesh. Affected populations include adults and children aged 4-60 years in India.
Event
India has approved its first dengue vaccine (QDENGA by Takeda Pharmaceutical) as cases surge in South Asia. The approval is confirmed by India’s drug regulator (CDSCO) based on phase III trial data.
Scale, impact and risk
Sri Lanka reported 76,044 cases and 53 deaths so far in 2026, with 2,652 new cases in a single day. Bangladesh reported over 9,000 cases and 30 deaths. India accounts for almost one-third of the global dengue burden. The disease places substantial strain on health systems.
Response, status and uncertainty
The vaccine is approved for adults and children aged 4-60 years and is WHO pre-qualified. Post-marketing safety and effectiveness studies are required within six months of commercial launch. The vaccine complements existing vector control measures but does not benefit pregnant women, newborns, or older adults.

新加坡登革热病例虽低但仍在增加,随着菌株的变化可能会进一步增加

Singapore dengue cases low but increasing, may rise further with strain change
新闻简报尽管与 2025 年同期相比,新加坡 2026 年上半年登革热病例总体下降了 54%,但新加坡登革热病例仍在增加。国家环境局报告了 1,180 例病例,4 月至 6 月大幅增加了 87.8%。这种激增与主要血清型从 DENV-2 到 DENV-3 的转变有关,这通常会因人群免疫力较低而导致更多感染。专家指出,虽然新加坡的 媒介 控制措施有助于缓解 暴发 的影响,但持续的 监测 和快速反应至关重要。马来西亚和越南的区域趋势也经历了 DENV-3 病例的激增,凸显了新加坡进一步增加的潜力。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表日期:2026-07-29。事件期间:2026年上半年(1月至6月),高峰季节为5月至10月。
地点与对象
新加坡,特别关注普通民众。区域背景包括马来西亚和越南。
事件
据专家和国家环境局 (NEA) 报告,新加坡登革热病例已确认增加,主要血清型从 DENV-2 转变为 DENV-3。
规模、影响与风险
2026 年上半年报告病例 1,180 例(比 2025 年减少 54%),但 4 月至 6 月(770 例)增加 87.8%。由于血清型变化和区域趋势而进一步上升的风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
媒介 控制措施(Wolbachia 项目)减少了 暴发,但需要持续的 监测 和快速响应。根据基因组序列预测流行病仍然存在不确定性。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Singapore dengue cases low but increasing, may rise further with strain change

News Brief
Singapore is experiencing a rise in dengue cases despite an overall 54% decrease in the first half of 2026 compared to the same period in 2025. The National Environment Agency reported 1,180 cases, with a significant 87.8% increase from April to June. This surge is linked to a shift in the dominant serotype from DENV-2 to DENV-3, which typically leads to more infections due to lower population immunity. Experts note that while Singapore's vector control measures have helped mitigate outbreaks, continued surveillance and rapid response are crucial. Regional trends in Malaysia and Vietnam, also experiencing spikes in DENV-3 cases, highlight the potential for further increases in Singapore.
Original source text (English)
Singapore dengue cases low but increasing, may rise further with strain change Sign up now: Get ST's newsletters delivered to your inbox There were 1,180 reported cases of dengue in Singapore in the first half of 2026. PHOTO: ST FILE Zhaki Abdullah Dengue cases in Singapore dropped 54% in the first half of 2026 but rose 87.8% from April to June amid a shift to the DENV-3 serotype. A change in dominant dengue serotype often leads to more infections due to lower immunity, seen also in Malaysia and Vietnam with rising cases. Experts say Singapore’s vector control helps reduce outbreaks, but continued surveillance and rapid response are vital to manage rising dengue numbers. AI generated SINGAPORE – While dengue numbers in Singapore remain low, a change in the dominant serotype could lead to increasing infections here, said experts. According to data from the National Environment Agency (NEA), there were 1,180 reported cases of dengue here in the first half of 2026 – 54 per cent down from the 2,583 cases over the same period in 2025. Dengue cases in Singapore have fallen in recent years amid an expansion of Project Wolbachia, an initiative to control the Aedes aegypti mosquito population here. However, infections have been creeping up as the country experiences peak dengue season, which lasts from May to October. There were 770 dengue cases recorded between April and June, an 87.8 per cent increase from the 410 seen in the first quarter of the year. This comes amid a change in the predominant dengue serotype in the Republic. The DENV-3 serotype – which made up just 19.1 per cent of cases in January – comprised 59.9 per cent of infections here in June, according to the NEA’s quarterly dengue surveillance data, published on July 21. There are four dengue serotypes, or strains, which are distinct but closely related. People who are infected by one serotype can still be infected by one of the other three. A switch in the dominant serotype is typically followed by a spike in dengue cases, due to lower immunity to the new strain. Across the Causeway, a shift in serotypes has been cited as one reason for a spike in dengue infections . Earlier in the month, Malaysia’s Health Ministry said dengue numbers in the country had risen, with 42,848 recorded cases of the disease as at July 9 – a 34.8 per cent increase from the same period in 2025. The ministry said then that 34 people had died of dengue, up from 19 across the same period in 2025 . Health Minister Dzulkefly Ahmad said the ­DENV-3 serotype is now dominant in Malaysia. Meanwhile, Vietnam reported more than 50,000 cases of dengue fever in the first five months of 2026 – double the number of infections seen over the same period the previous year. A serotype switch is the most likely reason for such spikes, said Paul Tambyah, the immediate past president of the Asia Pacific Society of Clinical Microbiology and Infection. He noted that this is the case with most dengue spikes over the years, as well as other viral infections such as influenza and Covid-19, which regularly see new strains or variants. The spike in Malaysia has been concentrated around Selangor, Kuala Lumpur and Putrajaya, where almost two-thirds of infections have occurred. However, the constant movement of people up and down the peninsula could result in spikes in Johor and Singapore, said Tambyah, who is also professor of medicine at the National University of Singapore’s Yong Loo Lin School of Medicine. “Singapore has already seen a rise in the number of dengue cases in the last quarter and this is likely to continue,” said Tambyah. He noted, however, that this may not occur if the DENV-2 serotype – which made up 67.6 per cent of cases at the start of 2026 – once again becomes predominant. Such sudden switches have happened in the past, he said, though the reasons for such occurrences are unclear. More on this topic Ooi Eng Eong – a professor in the emerging infectious diseases programme at Duke-NUS Medical School – pointed out that Singapore’s vector control programme means reduced dengue transmission and a longer interval between major outbreaks. Hence, an outbreak in Malaysia may not necessarily translate into one in Singapore, he said. Ooi said that with all four dengue serotypes circulating in Singapore, outbreaks of the disease here cannot always be attributed to low population immunity to an emerging strain. Genetic changes in each of the four strains can affect their ability to spread, and several dengue outbreaks have now been found to be caused by such genetic attributes, he added. “However, our ability to predict epidemics based on the genomic sequence of the viruses is still very much in its infancy. Research to understand the genetic changes that affect epidemic spread of dengue viruses remains much needed,” said Ooi. Tambyah said that a large spike in cases can be avoided by using the “tried and true techniques” of rapid case detection and surveillance, as well as timely and appropriate vector control. “These have worked in the past and are likely to work well again despite the serotype switch – at least in moderating the rise that we have already seen.” More on this topic Zhaki Abdullah is a correspondent at The Straits Times. He is on the health beat, in addition to occasionally covering science, environmental, tech and Muslim affairs issues. See more on Dengue fever Infectious diseases Mosquito
